B.4
Proof of Proposition
A.4

We show Proposition
A.4
(ii) first.

Proof of Proposition
A.4
(ii).

Note that we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{O}^{\top}Z_{t}-Z^{0}_{t}=(\bm{Y}_{r}^{0\top}\bm{Y}_{r}^{0})^{-1}\bm{Y}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{e}_{t}+\underbrace{\left((\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{r}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{r})^{-1}\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{r}-(\bm{Y}_{r}^{0\top}\bm{Y}_{r}^{0})^{-1}\bm{Y}_{r}^{0}\right)\mathbf{e}_{t}}_{\coloneqq R_{1,t}}+\underbrace{\left((\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{r}^{\top}\widetilde{\bm{Y}}_{r})^{-1}\widetilde{\bm{Y}}^{\top}_{r}\bm{Y}^{0}_{r}-I_{r}\right)Z_{t}^{0}}_{\coloneqq R_{2,t}},

where
\mathbf{e}_{t}=[\epsilon_{1t},\dots,\epsilon_{Nt}]
.

Lemma B.13
.

Under the assumption for Proposition
A.4
(ii),

\displaystyle\left\|R_{1,t}\right\|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\frac{\kappa^{5/2}\sqrt{N}\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}(\log N)^{\nu}q_{2,t}}{\psi_{\min}^{3/2}}+\frac{r^{1/2}\kappa^{5/2}\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}N^{1/6}}{\psi_{\min}^{3/2}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\left.+\frac{\kappa^{2}N\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}(\log N)^{\nu}q_{3,t}}{\psi_{\min}^{5/2}}+\frac{\kappa^{2}\sqrt{N}\max\{N^{3/2},T^{3/2}\}(\log N)^{\nu/2}}{\psi_{\min}^{7/2}}\right).

Lemma B.14
.

Under the assumption for Proposition
A.4
(ii),

\displaystyle\left\|R_{2,t}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{\kappa^{11/2}q_{1,t}\max\{N,T\}}{\psi_{\min}^{3/2}}+\frac{r\kappa^{3/2}q_{1,t}}{\sqrt{\psi_{\min}}}\right).

Proof of Lemma
B.13
.

Basically, the proof is similar to that of Lemma
B.5
. Here, we only prove the parts where the proofs are different from those of Lemma
B.5
. First, we check the part deriving the bound of
\left\|\text{Cov}(\mathbf{e}_{t}|(\mathbf{e}_{s})_{s\in(\mathcal{N}_{\delta_{2}}(t))^{c}})\right\|
. In the general dependence case, we have by the conditional weak dependence assumption that

\displaystyle\left\|\text{Cov}(\mathbf{e}_{t}|(\mathbf{e}_{s})_{s\in(\mathcal{N}_{\delta_{2}}(t))^{c}})\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\max_{1\leq l\leq N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\left|\text{Cov}(\epsilon_{lt},\epsilon_{jt}|(\mathbf{e}_{s})_{s\in(\mathcal{N}_{\delta_{2}}(t))^{c}})\right|

\displaystyle=O_{p}\left(\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\text{Var}(\epsilon_{it}|(\mathbf{e}_{s})_{s\in(\mathcal{N}_{\delta_{2}}(t))^{c}})\right)

\displaystyle=O_{p}(N^{1/3}).

The last equation comes from the proof of Lemma
B.10
. Next, we show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta_{2}}(t)^{c}}(\epsilon_{is}\epsilon_{ik}-\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{is}\epsilon_{ik}])v_{k}^{0}\right\|^{2}\right|\bm{M}^{0}\right]\lesssim rN.

Define
\gamma_{ik}=\epsilon_{is}\epsilon_{ik}-\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{is}\epsilon_{ik}]
. In addition, let
\bm{\Gamma}=(\gamma_{it})_{i\leq N,t\leq T}
and
\bm{\Gamma}^{(-\mathcal{N}_{\delta_{2}}(t))}
be the matrix which replaces the columns in
\mathcal{N}_{\delta_{2}}(t)
of
\bm{\Gamma}
with zeros. Then, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta_{2}}(t)^{c}}\gamma_{ik}v_{k}^{0}\right\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{r}\left(\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta_{2}}(t)^{c}}\gamma_{ik}v_{kl}^{0}\right)^{2}

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{r}\mathbf{P}_{l}^{\top}vec\left(\left(\bm{\Gamma}^{(-\mathcal{N}_{\delta_{2}}(t))}\right)^{\top}\right)vec\left(\left(\bm{\Gamma}^{(-\mathcal{N}_{\delta_{2}}(t))}\right)^{\top}\right)^{\top}\mathbf{P}_{l},

where
\mathbf{P}_{l}=\left[(\mathbf{V}_{r,l}^{0})^{\top},\cdots,(\mathbf{V}_{r,l}^{0})^{\top}\right]^{\top}
is the
NT\times 1
vector which piles up the
N
number of
\mathbf{V}_{r,l}^{0}
s and
\mathbf{V}_{r,l}^{0}
is the
l
-th column of
\mathbf{V}_{r}^{0}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta_{2}}(t)^{c}}\gamma_{ik}v_{k}^{0}\right\|^{2}\right\|\bm{M}^{0}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{l=1}^{r}\mathbf{P}_{l}^{\top}\text{Cov}\left(vec\left(\left(\bm{\Gamma}^{(-\mathcal{N}_{\delta_{2}}(t))}\right)^{\top}\right)\right)\mathbf{P}_{l}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{l=1}^{r}\left\|\mathbf{P}_{l}\right\|^{2}\left\|Cov\left(vec\left(\left(\bm{\Gamma}^{(-\mathcal{N}_{\delta_{2}}(t))}\right)^{\top}\right)\right)\right\|.

Note that

\sum_{l=1}^{r}\left\|\mathbf{P}_{l}\right\|^{2}=\sum_{l=1}^{r}N\sum_{s=1}^{T}(v_{ls}^{0})^{2}=N\left\|\mathbf{V}_{r}^{0}\right\|_{F}^{2}\leq rN,

and

\left\|Cov\left(vec\left(\left(\bm{\Gamma}^{(-\mathcal{N}_{\delta_{2}}(t))}\right)^{\top}\right)\right)\right\|\leq\max_{1\leq i\leq N}\max_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta_{2}}(t)^{c}}\sum_{1\leq j\leq N}\sum_{q\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta_{2}}(t)^{c}}\left|\text{Cov}(\epsilon_{is}\epsilon_{ik},\epsilon_{js}\epsilon_{jq})\right|\leq C

for some constant
C>0
by Assumption B”’. Hence, we have the desired result. Except for the above parts, the proof is same as that of Lemma
B.5
.

Proof of Lemma
B.14
.

This proof is symmetric to the proof of Lemma
B.2
except for the part where we show
\left\|\bm{Y}_{r}^{0\top}\bm{E}\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0\top}\right\|_{F}=O_{p}\left(r\kappa\psi_{\min}\right)
. Note that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left.\left\|\bm{Y}_{r}^{0\top}\bm{E}\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0\top}\right\|_{F}^{2}\right|\bm{M}^{0}\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{r}\sum_{l=1}^{r}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{s=1}^{T}\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{it}\epsilon_{js}\right]Y_{i,k}^{0}Z_{t,l}^{0}Y_{j,k}^{0}Z_{s,l}^{0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=1}^{r}\sum_{l=1}^{r}\mathbf{A}_{k,l}^{\top}\text{Cov}\left(vec(\bm{E})\right)\mathbf{A}_{k,l}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=1}^{r}\sum_{l=1}^{r}\left\|\mathbf{A}_{k,l}\right\|^{2}\left\|\text{Cov}\left(vec(\bm{E})\right)\right\|

\displaystyle=\left\|\bm{Y}_{r}^{0}\right\|_{F}^{2}\left\|\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0}\right\|_{F}^{2}\left\|\text{Cov}\left(vec(\bm{E})\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\lesssim r^{2}\kappa^{2}\psi_{\min}^{2},

where

\mathbf{A}_{k,l}=\left[Y_{1,k}^{0}Z_{1,l}^{0},Y_{2,k}^{0}Z_{1,l}^{0},\cdots,Y_{N,k}^{0}Z_{1,l}^{0},Y_{1,k}^{0}Z_{2,l}^{0},\cdots,,Y_{N,k}^{0}Z_{2,l}^{0},Y_{1,k}^{0}Z_{3,l}^{0},\cdots,Y_{N,k}^{0}Z_{T,l}^{0}\right]
is the
NT\times 1
vector. Here, we use the fact that

\sum_{k=1}^{r}\sum_{l=1}^{r}\left\|\mathbf{A}_{k,l}\right\|^{2}=\sum_{k=1}^{r}\sum_{l=1}^{r}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{t=1}^{T}(Y_{i,k}^{0})^{2}(Z_{t,l}^{0})^{2}=\left\|\bm{Y}_{r}^{0}\right\|_{F}^{2}\left\|\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0}\right\|_{F}^{2}.

Proof of Proposition
A.4
(i).

The proof is symmetric to that of Proposition
A.4
(ii). Hence, we omit it.
\square

Appendix C
Proofs of Theorems and Lemma in the Main Text

C.1
Proofs of Theorems
3.1
and
3.2

Proof of Theorem
3.2
.

First, we prove Theorem
3.2
. Note that by Assumptions A(i) - (ii),
||T^{-1/2}\bm{F}^{0}||
,
\psi_{r}(T^{-1/2}\bm{F}^{0})
,
||N^{-\alpha/2}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}||
, and
\psi_{r}(N^{-\alpha/2}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})
converge (in probability) to certain deterministic positive constants, respectively. Hence, w.h.p., there are constants
c,C>0
such that

\displaystyle c

\displaystyle<\psi_{r}(N^{-\alpha/2}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})\psi_{r}(T^{-1/2}\bm{F}^{0})\leq N^{-\alpha/2}T^{-1/2}\psi_{r}(\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\bm{F}^{0\top})=N^{-\alpha/2}T^{-1/2}\psi_{\min}

\displaystyle\leq N^{-\alpha/2}T^{-1/2}\psi_{\max}=N^{-\alpha/2}T^{-1/2}\left\|\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\bm{F}^{0\top}\right\|\leq\left\|N^{-\alpha/2}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\right\|\left\|T^{-1/2}\bm{F}^{0}\right\|<C.

Then, because we have
cN^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}\leq\psi_{\min}\leq\psi_{\max}\leq CN^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}
w.h.p., we can replace
\psi_{\min}
in Assumption F with
N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}
, and
\kappa
is bounded. In addition, since
u_{i}^{0}=\mathbf{G}^{\top}(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{-1/2}\lambda^{0}_{i}
where
\mathbf{G}
is an eigenvectors matrix of
\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}}{N^{\alpha}}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0}}{T}\right)\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}}{N^{\alpha}}\right)^{1/2}
as noted in the proof of Lemma
C.1
, we have

\left\|u_{i}^{0}\right\|\leq\left\|(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{-1/2}\right\|\left\|\lambda^{0}_{i}\right\|=O_{p}\left(N^{(\alpha_{i}-\alpha-1)/2}\right)

by the assumption that
\left\|\lambda^{0}_{i}\right\|\leq CN^{\frac{\alpha_{i}-1}{2}}
. So, we can set
\rho_{i}
in Assumptions E(i) and F(i) to
N^{(\alpha_{i}-\alpha-1)/2}
. Then, Assumptions E(i) and F(i) are satisfied under Assumptions A and D(i). Similarly, because

\left\|v_{t}^{0}\right\|\leq\left\|(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0})^{-1/2}\right\|\left\|f^{0}_{t}\right\|=O_{p}\left(T^{-1/2}\right),

we can set
q_{t}
in Assumptions E(ii) and F(ii) to
T^{-1/2}
. Then, Assumptions E(ii) and F(ii) are satisfied under Assumptions A and D(ii).

Proof of Theorem
3.2
(i) and (ii).

Using Lemma
C.1
, we can get the following decomposition from Proposition
A.1
(i):

\displaystyle\sqrt{T}(\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-\mathbf{H}^{-1}\lambda_{i}^{0})=\sqrt{T}\mathbf{H}^{-1}(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0})^{-1}\bm{F}^{0\top}\mathbf{e}_{i}+\mathbf{D}_{r}^{1/2}\mathbf{O}\mathcal{R}_{y,i}

(11)

where
\mathbf{H}=(\mathbf{D}_{r}^{1/2}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{H}^{0})^{-1}
. Then, by Assumptions A, C, Lemmas
C.2
, and
C.3
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{T}\mathbf{H}^{-1}(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0})^{-1}\bm{F}^{0\top}\mathbf{e}_{i}

\displaystyle=\left(\frac{\mathbf{D}_{r}^{1/2}}{N^{\alpha/4}T^{1/4}}\right)\mathbf{O}\left(N^{\alpha/4}T^{1/4}\mathbf{H}^{0}\right)\left(\frac{\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0}}{T}\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\epsilon_{it}f_{t}^{0}

\displaystyle\to_{d}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\mathcal{I}_{sgn}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\mathcal{G}^{*\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}^{-1}\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{F},i}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1/2}\mathcal{G}^{*}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\mathcal{I}_{sgn}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\right).

Since
\mathcal{I}_{sgn}
and
\mathcal{D}^{1/2}
are diagonal matrices, we know
\mathcal{I}_{sgn}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}=\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\mathcal{I}_{sgn}
. In addition,
\mathcal{G}^{*}\mathcal{I}_{sgn}
is also an eigenvector of
\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}
which has a different column sign from
\mathcal{G}^{*}
because the diagonal elements of
\mathcal{I}_{sgn}
consists of
\pm 1
. In fact, as noted in the proofs of Lemmas
C.2
and
C.3
, the column sign of
\mathcal{G}^{*}
is determined by the sign alignment between
\bm{\Lambda}^{0}
and
\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0}
, and the sign of
\mathcal{I}_{sgn}
is determined by the sign alignment between
\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0}
and
\mathbf{U}_{r}
. Hence,
\mathcal{G}\coloneqq\mathcal{G}^{*}\mathcal{I}_{sgn}
is the eigenvector of
\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}
whose column sign is determined by the sign alignment between
\bm{\Lambda}^{0}
and
\mathbf{U}_{r}
. Hence, given
\bm{\Lambda}^{0}
and
\mathbf{U}_{r}
, it is determined. Then, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}\left(0,\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\mathcal{I}_{sgn}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\mathcal{G}^{*\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}^{-1}\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{F},i}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1/2}\mathcal{G}^{*}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\mathcal{I}_{sgn}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\mathcal{D}\mathcal{G}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}^{-1}\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{F},i}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1/2}\mathcal{G}\mathcal{D}\right).

In addition, from the relation
\mathcal{D}^{2}=\mathcal{G}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}\mathcal{G}
, we have
\mathcal{G}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}^{-1}=\mathcal{D}^{-2}\mathcal{G}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}
. Hence,

\displaystyle\mathcal{N}\left(0,\mathcal{D}\mathcal{G}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}^{-1}\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{F},i}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}^{-1}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1/2}\mathcal{G}\mathcal{D}\right)\overset{d}{=}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\mathcal{D}^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{F},i}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}\mathcal{G}\mathcal{D}^{-1}\right)\overset{d}{=}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\mathcal{Q}^{-\top}\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{F},i}\mathcal{Q}^{-1}\right),

where
\mathcal{Q}^{-\top}=\mathcal{D}^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}
. In addition, we have
||\mathbf{D}_{r}^{1/2}||||\mathbf{O}||||\mathcal{R}_{y,i}||=o_{p}(1)
from Proposition
A.1
(i) since
\psi_{\max}\asymp N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}
and
||\mathbf{D}_{r}^{1/2}||\asymp N^{\alpha/4}T^{1/4}
.

Next, we show the asymptotic normality of
\widehat{f}_{t}
. Using Lemma
C.1
, we can get the following decomposition from Proposition
A.1
(ii):

\displaystyle\sqrt{N^{\alpha}}\left(\widehat{f}_{t}-\mathbf{H}^{\top}f_{t}^{0}\right)=\sqrt{N^{\alpha}}\mathbf{H}^{\top}(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\mathbf{e}_{t}+\sqrt{N^{\alpha}T}\mathbf{D}_{r}^{-1/2}\mathbf{O}\mathcal{R}_{z,t}

(12)

where
\mathbf{H}=(\mathbf{D}_{r}^{1/2}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{H}^{0})^{-1}
. Then, by Assumptions A, C, Lemmas
C.2
, and
C.3
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{N^{\alpha}}\mathbf{H}^{\top}(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\mathbf{e}_{t}

\displaystyle=\left(N^{\alpha/4}T^{1/4}\mathbf{D}_{r}^{-1/2}\right)\mathbf{O}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\alpha/4}T^{1/4}}(\mathbf{H}^{0})^{-\top}\right)\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}}{N^{\alpha}}\right)^{-1}\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{\alpha}}}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\epsilon_{is}\lambda^{0}_{i}

\displaystyle\to_{d}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\mathcal{D}^{-1/2}\mathcal{I}_{sgn}\mathcal{D}^{-1/2}\mathcal{G}^{*\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1/2}\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{\Lambda},t}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1/2}\mathcal{G}^{*}\mathcal{D}^{-1/2}\mathcal{I}_{sgn}\mathcal{D}^{-1/2}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\mathcal{D}^{-1}\mathcal{G}^{\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1/2}\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{\Lambda},t}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1/2}\mathcal{G}\mathcal{D}^{-1}\right)

\displaystyle\overset{d}{=}\mathcal{N}\left(0,\mathcal{D}^{-2}\mathcal{Q}\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{\Lambda},t}\mathcal{Q}^{\top}\mathcal{D}^{-2}\right).

In addition, we have
\sqrt{N^{\alpha}T}||\mathbf{D}_{r}^{-1/2}||||\mathbf{O}||||\mathcal{R}_{z,t}||=o_{p}(1)
from Proposition
A.1
(ii) since
\psi_{\max}\asymp\sqrt{N^{\alpha}T}
and
||\mathbf{D}_{r}^{1/2}||\asymp N^{\alpha/4}T^{1/4}
.
\square

Proof of Theorem
3.2
(iii).

We have the following decomposition:

\displaystyle\widehat{m}_{it}-m^{0}_{it}

\displaystyle=\widehat{f}_{t}^{\top}\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-m^{0}_{it}

\displaystyle=f_{t}^{0\top}\left(\mathbf{H}\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{0}\right)+\left(\mathbf{H}^{-\top}\widehat{f}_{t}-f_{t}^{0}\right)^{\top}\lambda_{i}^{0}+\left(\mathbf{H}^{-\top}\widehat{f}_{t}-f_{t}^{0}\right)^{\top}\left(\mathbf{H}\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{0}\right)

\displaystyle=f_{t}^{0\top}\left(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0}\right)^{-1}\sum_{s=1}^{T}f_{s}^{0}\epsilon_{is}+\lambda_{i}^{0\top}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\right)^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\lambda^{0}_{j}\epsilon_{jt}+\sum_{k=1}^{3}\Delta_{k,it},

where the residual terms are

\displaystyle\Delta_{1,it}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}f_{t}^{0\top}(\mathbf{H}^{0})^{-1}\mathcal{R}_{y,i},\ \ \Delta_{2,it}=\sqrt{T}\lambda_{i}^{0\top}(\mathbf{H}^{0})^{\top}\mathcal{R}_{z,t},\ \ \Delta_{3,it}=\left(\mathbf{H}^{-\top}\widehat{f}_{t}-f_{t}^{0}\right)^{\top}\left(\mathbf{H}\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-\lambda_{i}^{0}\right).

Furthermore, we have
\left\|\mathcal{R}_{y,i}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{N^{(\alpha_{i}-\alpha-1)/2}\max\{N,T\}}{(N^{\alpha}T)^{3/4}}+\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{(N^{\alpha}T)^{3/4}}\right)
by Proposition
A.1
(i) since w.h.p.,
\psi_{\min}\asymp N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}
and
\kappa=O_{p}(1)
. So, we have by Lemma
C.2
(iii) that

\left\|\Delta_{1,it}\right\|\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left\|f_{t}^{0}\right\|\left\|(\mathbf{H}^{0})^{-1}\right\|\left\|\mathcal{R}_{y,i}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{i}-1}{2}}\max\{N,T\}}{N^{\alpha}T}+\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{N^{\alpha/2}T}\right).

Similarly, we have
\left\|\mathcal{R}_{z,t}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{\max\{N,T\}}{(N^{\alpha}T)^{3/4}\sqrt{T}}+\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{(N^{\alpha}T)^{3/4}}\right)
from Proposition
A.1
(ii). Hence, we have by Lemma
C.2
(iii) that

\left\|\Delta_{2,it}\right\|\leq\sqrt{T}\left\|\lambda_{i}^{0}\right\|\left\|\mathbf{H}^{0}\right\|\left\|\mathcal{R}_{z,t}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{i}-1}{2}}\max\{N,T\}}{N^{\alpha}T}+\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{i}-1}{2}}\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{N^{\alpha}T^{1/2}}\right).

In addition, the terms in
\Delta_{3,it}
can be bounded like

\displaystyle\left\|\left((\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{-1}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}^{0}\epsilon_{it}\right)^{\top}(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0})^{-1}\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_{t}^{0}\epsilon_{it}\right\|\leq\left\|(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{-1}\right\|\left\|(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0})^{-1}\right\|\left\|\sum_{i=1}^{N}\lambda_{i}^{0}\epsilon_{it}\right\|\left\|\sum_{t=1}^{T}f_{t}^{0}\epsilon_{it}\right\|

\displaystyle\quad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}}\right),

\displaystyle\left\|(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\lambda^{0}_{j}\epsilon_{jt}\right\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\left\|(\mathbf{H}^{0})^{-1}\mathcal{R}_{y,i}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{i}-1}{2}}\max\{N,T\}}{N^{3\alpha/2}T}+\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{N^{\alpha}T}\right),

\displaystyle\left\|(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0})^{-1}\sum_{s=1}^{T}f_{s}^{0}\epsilon_{is}\right\|\sqrt{T}\left\|(\mathbf{H}^{0})^{\top}\mathcal{R}_{z,t}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{\max\{N,T\}}{N^{\alpha}T^{3/2}}+\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{N^{\alpha}T}\right).

Next, we derive the bound of
\mathcal{V}_{it}^{-1}
. Note that w.h.p.,

\left\|N^{-\alpha}\lambda_{i}^{0\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1}\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{\Lambda},t}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1}\lambda_{i}^{0}\right\|\geq N^{-\alpha}\left\|\lambda_{i}^{0}\right\|^{2}\lambda_{\min}\left(\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1}\right)^{2}\lambda_{\min}(\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{\Lambda},t})\geq\frac{c}{N^{(1+\alpha-\alpha_{i})}},

for some constant
c>0
, because
\left\|\lambda_{i}^{0}\right\|\geq cN^{(\alpha_{i}-1)/2}
. Similarly, we have
\left\|T^{-1}f_{t}^{0\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}^{-1}\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{F},i}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}^{-1}f_{t}^{0}\right\|\geq\frac{c}{T}
for some constant
c>0
w.h.p. Therefore, we have

\mathcal{V}_{it}^{-1}=\frac{1}{N^{-\alpha}\lambda_{i}^{0\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1}\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{\Lambda},t}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1}\lambda_{i}^{0}+T^{-1}f_{t}^{0\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}^{-1}\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{F},i}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}^{-1}f_{t}^{0}}=O_{p}\left(\min\{N^{(1+\alpha-\alpha_{i})},T\}\right),

and we can check that
\mathcal{V}_{it}^{-1/2}\left\|\sum_{k=1}^{3}\Delta_{k,it}\right\|=o_{p}(1)
under our assumptions. Lastly, we can show that
\mathcal{V}_{it}^{-1/2}\left(f_{t}^{0\top}\left(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0}\right)^{-1}\sum_{s=1}^{T}f_{s}^{0}\epsilon_{is}+\lambda_{i}^{0\top}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\right)^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\lambda_{j}^{0}\epsilon_{jt}\right)\to_{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)
by the same assertion as in the proof of Theorem 3 in
Bai, 2003
. It completes the proof.
\square

Proof of Theorem
3.1
.

First, we check the following conditions of Proposition
A.1
:

\kappa\rho_{i}\to_{p}0,\qquad\kappa q_{t}\to_{p}0,\qquad\text{and}\qquad\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{\psi_{\min}}\to_{p}0.

As noted above, we can replace
\psi_{\min}
,
\rho_{i}
,
q_{t}
with
N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}
,
N^{(\alpha_{i}-\alpha-1)/2}
,
T^{-1/2}
, respectively. In addition, w.h.p.,
\kappa
is bounded. Hence, we have w.h.p.,

\displaystyle\kappa\rho_{i}\lesssim N^{(\alpha_{i}-\alpha-1)/2}\leq N^{-\alpha/2}\rightarrow 0,\quad\kappa q_{t}\lesssim T^{-1/2}\rightarrow 0,\quad\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{\psi_{\min}}\asymp\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}}\rightarrow 0.

Hence, the above conditions of Proposition
A.1
are satisfied, and we can use the bounds of
\left\|\mathcal{R}_{y,i}\right\|
and
\left\|\mathcal{R}_{z,t}\right\|
in Proposition
A.1
by replacing
\psi_{\min}
,
\rho_{i}
,
q_{t}
with
N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}
,
N^{(\alpha_{i}-\alpha-1)/2}
,
T^{-1/2}
. Note that

\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-\mathbf{H}^{-1}\lambda_{i}^{0}=\mathbf{H}^{-1}(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0})^{-1}\bm{F}^{0\top}\mathbf{e}_{i}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{D}_{r}^{1/2}\mathbf{O}\mathcal{R}_{y,i}.

By Lemma
C.2
,
\mathbf{H}^{-1}=\mathbf{D}_{r}^{1/2}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{H}^{0}=O_{p}(1)
,
\mathbf{D}_{r}^{1/2}=O_{p}(N^{\alpha/4}T^{1/4})
, and
(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0})^{-1}\bm{F}^{0\top}\mathbf{e}_{i}=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)
. Hence, we have from Proposition
A.1
(i) that

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-\mathbf{H}^{-1}\lambda_{i}^{0}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}+\sqrt{\frac{N^{\alpha_{i}}}{N}}\frac{\max\{N,T\}}{N^{\alpha}T}\right).

Note that

\displaystyle\widehat{f}_{t}-\mathbf{H}^{\top}f_{t}^{0}=\mathbf{H}^{\top}(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\mathbf{e}_{t}+\sqrt{T}\mathbf{D}_{r}^{-1/2}\mathbf{O}\mathcal{R}_{z,t}.

By Lemma
C.2
,
\mathbf{H}^{\top}=\mathbf{D}_{r}^{-1/2}\mathbf{O}(\mathbf{H}^{0})^{-\top}=O_{p}(1)
,
\mathbf{D}_{r}^{-1/2}=O_{p}(1/N^{\alpha/4}T^{1/4})
, and
(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\mathbf{e}_{t}=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)
. Similarly, we can have from Proposition
A.1
(ii) that

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{f}_{t}-\mathbf{H}^{\top}f_{t}^{0}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{\alpha}}}+\frac{\max\{N,T\}}{N^{\alpha}T}\right).

In addition, the bound of
||\widehat{m}_{it}-m^{0}_{it}||
can be easily derived from the relation

\left\|\widehat{m}_{it}-m^{0}_{it}\right\|\leq\left\|\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-\mathbf{H}^{-1}\lambda_{i}^{0}\right\|\left\|\mathbf{H}^{\top}f_{t}^{0}\right\|+\left\|\widehat{f}_{t}-\mathbf{H}^{\top}f_{t}^{0}\right\|\left\|\mathbf{H}^{-1}\lambda_{i}^{0}\right\|+\left\|\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-\mathbf{H}^{-1}\lambda_{i}^{0}\right\|\left\|\widehat{f}_{t}-\mathbf{H}^{\top}f_{t}^{0}\right\|.

Hence, we omit it here.
\square

C.2
Proof of Theorems
4.2
and
4.3

Proof of Theorem
4.3
(i) and (ii).

First, we prove Theorem
4.3
. As noted in Section
C.1
, we can replace
\psi_{\min}
,
\rho_{i}
,
q_{1,t}
in Assumptions E’ and F’ with
N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}
,
N^{(\alpha_{i}-\alpha-1)/2}
,
T^{-1/2}
, respectively. In addition, because
\left\|v_{s}^{0}\right\|\leq\left\|(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0})^{-1/2}\right\|\left\|f_{s}^{0}\right\|
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)|}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)}\left\|v_{s}^{0}\right\|^{2}\leq\left\|(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0})^{-1/2}\right\|^{2}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)|}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)}\left\|f_{s}^{0}\right\|^{2}=O_{p}\left(T^{-1}\right)

by Assumption A(iv). So, we can set
q_{2,t}
in Assumptions E’(ii) and F’(ii) to
T^{-1/2}
. Moreover, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)|N}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)^{c}}|\text{Cov}(\epsilon_{is},\epsilon_{ik})|\left\|v_{k}^{0}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0})^{-1/2}\right\|\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)|N}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)^{c}}|\text{Cov}(\epsilon_{is},\epsilon_{ik})|\left\|f_{k}^{0}\right\|=O_{p}\left(T^{-1/2}\right),

because by Assumption B’,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)|N}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)^{c}}|\text{Cov}(\epsilon_{is},\epsilon_{ik})|\mathbb{E}[\left\|f_{k}^{0}\right\|]

\displaystyle\ \ \leq\frac{C_{1}}{|\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)|N}\sum_{s\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)}\sum_{i=1}^{N}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)^{c}}|\text{Cov}(\epsilon_{is},\epsilon_{ik})|\leq C_{1}\max_{s\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)}\max_{i\leq N}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)^{c}}|\text{Cov}(\epsilon_{is},\epsilon_{ik})|\leq C_{2},

for some constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
. Hence, we can set
q_{3,t}
in Assumptions E’(ii) and F’(ii) to
T^{-1/2}
. Then, the assumptions of Proposition
A.2
are satisfied under the assumptions of Theorem
4.3
, once we replace
\psi_{\min}
,
\rho_{i}
,
q_{1,t}
,
q_{2,t}
,
q_{3,t}
with
N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}
,
N^{(\alpha_{i}-\alpha-1)/2}
,
T^{-1/2}
,
T^{-1/2}
,
T^{-1/2}
, respectively. Therefore, we can use the result of Proposition
A.2
. Except for this discussion, the proofs of Theorem
4.3
(i) and (ii) are the same as those of Theorem
3.2
(i) and (ii) in Section
C.1
. Hence, we omit it.
\square

Proof of Theorem
4.3
(iii).

The way of proof is basically the same as that of Theorem
3.2
(iii). By the same token as in the proof of Theorem
3.2
(iii) with the aid of Proposition
A.2
, we can derive

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{1,it}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{i}-1}{2}}\max\{N,T\}}{N^{\alpha}T}+\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{N^{\alpha/2}T}\right),

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{2,it}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{i}}{2}}\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}(\log N)^{\nu}}{N^{\alpha}T}+\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{i}-1}{2}}\max\{N,T\}}{N^{\alpha}T}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{i}-1}{2}}\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}N^{1/6}}{N^{\alpha}T^{1/2}}+\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{i}}{2}}\max\{N^{3/2},T^{3/2}\}(\log N)^{\nu/2}}{N^{2\alpha}T^{3/2}}\right),

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{3,it}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}}+\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{i}-1}{2}}\max\{N,T\}}{N^{3\alpha/2}T}+\frac{\max\{N,T\}N^{1/6}}{N^{\alpha}T^{3/2}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\frac{\sqrt{N}\max\{N^{3/2},T^{3/2}\}(\log N)^{\nu/2}}{N^{2\alpha}T^{2}}+\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{i}-1}{2}}\max\{N^{2},T^{2}\}N^{1/6}}{N^{2\alpha}T^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{i}}{2}}\max\{N^{5/2},T^{5/2}\}(\log N)^{\nu/2}}{N^{3\alpha}T^{5/2}}\right).

In addition, we have from the proof of Theorem
3.2
(iii) that

\mathcal{V}_{it}^{-1}=\frac{1}{N^{-\alpha}\lambda_{i}^{0\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1}\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{\Lambda},t}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1}\lambda_{i}^{0}+T^{-1}f_{t}^{0\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}^{-1}\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{F},i}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}^{-1}f_{t}^{0}}=O_{p}\left(\min\{N^{(1+\alpha-\alpha_{i})},T\}\right).

So,
\mathcal{V}_{it}^{-1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\Delta_{k,it}=o_{p}(1)
under our assumptions. Lastly, we can show that

\mathcal{V}_{it}^{-1/2}\left(f_{t}^{0\top}\left(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0}\right)^{-1}\sum_{s=1}^{T}f_{s}^{0}\epsilon_{is}+\lambda_{i}^{0\top}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\right)^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\lambda_{j}^{0}\epsilon_{jt}\right)\to_{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)

by the same assertion as in the proof of Theorem 3 in
Bai, 2003
. It completes the proof.
\square

Proof of Theorem
4.2
.

First, we check the following conditions of Proposition
A.2
:

\kappa\rho_{i}\to_{p}0,\qquad(\log N)^{\nu/2}\kappa q_{2,t}\to_{p}0,\qquad\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{\psi_{\min}}\to_{p}0.

As noted above, we can replace
\psi_{\min}
,
\rho_{i}
,
q_{2,t}
with
N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}
,
N^{(\alpha_{i}-\alpha-1)/2}
,
T^{-1/2}
, respectively. In addition, w.h.p.,
\kappa
is bounded. Hence, we have w.h.p.,

\displaystyle\kappa\rho_{i}\lesssim N^{(\alpha_{i}-\alpha-1)/2}\leq N^{(-\alpha/2)}\rightarrow 0,\qquad\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{\psi_{\min}}\asymp\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}}\rightarrow 0,

\displaystyle(\log N)^{\nu/2}\kappa q_{2,t}\lesssim(\log N)^{\nu/2}T^{-1/2}\rightarrow 0.

Then, the above conditions of Proposition
A.2
are satisfied. In addition, as noted above, we can set
\rho_{i}
,
q_{1,t}
,
q_{2,t}
,
q_{3,t}
in Assumption E’ to
N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}
,
N^{(\alpha_{i}-\alpha-1)/2}
,
T^{-1/2}
,
T^{-1/2}
,
T^{-1/2}
, respectively. Hence, we can use the bounds of
\left\|\mathcal{R}_{y,i}\right\|
and
\left\|\mathcal{R}_{z,t}\right\|
in Proposition
A.2
after replacing
\psi_{\min}
,
\rho_{i}
,
q_{1,t}
,
q_{2,t}
,
q_{3,t}
with
N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}
,
N^{(\alpha_{i}-\alpha-1)/2}
,
T^{-1/2}
,
T^{-1/2}
,
T^{-1/2}
, respectively. Note that

\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-\mathbf{H}^{-1}\lambda_{i}^{0}=\mathbf{H}^{-1}(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0})^{-1}\bm{F}^{0\top}\mathbf{e}_{i}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\mathbf{D}_{r}^{1/2}\mathbf{O}\mathcal{R}_{y,i}.

By Lemma
C.2
,
\mathbf{H}^{-1}=\mathbf{D}_{r}^{1/2}\mathbf{O}\mathbf{H}^{0}=O_{p}(1)
,
\mathbf{D}_{r}^{1/2}=O_{p}(N^{\alpha/4}T^{1/4})
, and
(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0})^{-1}\bm{F}^{0\top}\mathbf{e}_{i}=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}\right)
. Hence, we have from Proposition
A.2
(i) that

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-\mathbf{H}^{-1}\lambda_{i}^{0}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}+\sqrt{\frac{N^{\alpha_{i}}}{N}}\frac{\max\{N,T\}}{N^{\alpha}T}\right).

In addition, because
\widehat{f}_{t}-\mathbf{H}^{\top}f_{t}^{0}=\mathbf{H}^{\top}(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{-1}\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\mathbf{e}_{t}+\sqrt{T}\mathbf{D}_{r}^{-1/2}\mathbf{O}\mathcal{R}_{z,t}
, we can similarly have from Proposition
A.2
(ii) that

\displaystyle\left\|\widehat{f}_{t}-\mathbf{H}^{\top}f_{t}^{0}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{N^{\alpha}}}+\frac{\max\{N,T\}N^{1/6}}{N^{\alpha}T}+\frac{\sqrt{N}\max\{N^{3/2},T^{3/2}\}(\log N)^{\nu}}{N^{2\alpha}T^{3/2}}\right).

In addition, the bound of
||\widehat{m}_{it}-m^{0}_{it}||
can easily be derived from the bounds of
||\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-\mathbf{H}^{-1}\lambda_{i}^{0}||
and
||\widehat{f}_{t}-\mathbf{H}^{\top}f_{t}^{0}||
. Hence, we omit it.
\square

and

Proof of Theorem
4.5
(i) and (ii).

, we can replace
\psi_{\min}
,
\rho_{1,i}
,

,
N^{(\alpha_{1,i}-\alpha-1)/2}
,
T^{-1/2}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(i)|}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(i)}\left\|u_{j}^{0}\right\|^{2}\leq\left\|(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{-1/2}\right\|^{2}\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(i)|}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(i)}\left\|\lambda_{j}^{0}\right\|^{2}=O_{p}\left(N^{\alpha_{2,i}-\alpha-1}\right)

\rho_{2,i}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(i)|T}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(i)}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(i)^{c}}|\text{Cov}(\epsilon_{jt},\epsilon_{kt})|\left\|u_{k}^{0}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\left\|(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{-1/2}\right\|\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(i)|T}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(i)}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(i)^{c}}|\text{Cov}(\epsilon_{jt},\epsilon_{kt})|\left\|\lambda_{k}^{0}\right\|=O_{p}\left(N^{-\alpha/2}\right),

because by Assumption B”,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(i)|T}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(i)}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(i)^{c}}|\text{Cov}(\epsilon_{jt},\epsilon_{kt})|\left\|\lambda_{k}^{0}\right\|

\displaystyle\ \ \leq\frac{C_{1}}{|\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(i)|T}\sum_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(i)}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(i)^{c}}|\text{Cov}(\epsilon_{jt},\epsilon_{kt})|\leq C_{1}\max_{j\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(i)}\max_{t\leq T}\sum_{k\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(i)^{c}}|\text{Cov}(\epsilon_{jt},\epsilon_{kt})|\leq C_{2},

for some constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
. Hence, we can set
\rho_{3,i}
in Assumptions E”(i) and F”(i) to
N^{-\alpha/2}
. Then, the assumptions of Proposition
A.3
are satisfied under the assumptions of Theorem
4.5
, once we replace
\psi_{\min}
,
q_{t}
,
\rho_{1,i}
,
\rho_{2,i}
,
\rho_{3,i}
with
N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}
,
T^{-1/2}
,
N^{(\alpha_{1,i}-\alpha-1)/2}
,
N^{(\alpha_{2,i}-\alpha-1)/2}
,

, respectively. Therefore, we can use the result of Proposition

. Except for this discussion, the proofs of Theorem

(i) and (ii) in Section

. Hence, we omit it.
\square

Proof of Theorem
4.5
(iii).

The way of proof is basically the same as that of Theorem
3.2
(iii). By the same token as in the proof of Theorem
3.2
(iii) with the aid of Proposition
A.3
, we can derive

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{1,it}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{2,i}-1}{2}}\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}(\log N)^{\omega}}{N^{\alpha}T^{1/2}}+\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{1,i}-1}{2}}\max\{N,T\}}{N^{\alpha}T}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}T^{1/6}}{N^{\alpha/2}T}+\frac{\max\{N^{3/2},T^{3/2}\}(\log N)^{\omega/2}}{N^{3\alpha/2}T^{3/2}}\right)\coloneqq O_{p}(A),

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{2,it}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{1,i}-1}{2}}\max\{N,T\}}{N^{\alpha}T}+\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{1,i}-1}{2}}\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{N^{\alpha}T^{1/2}}\right),

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{3,it}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{1}{N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}}+\frac{\max\{N,T\}}{N^{\alpha}T^{3/2}}+A\right).

In addition, we have from the proof of Theorem
3.2
(iii) that

\mathcal{V}_{it}^{-1}=\frac{1}{N^{-\alpha}\lambda_{i}^{0\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1}\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{\Lambda},t}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1}\lambda_{i}^{0}+T^{-1}f_{t}^{0\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}^{-1}\bm{\Phi}_{\bm{F},i}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}^{-1}f_{t}^{0}}=O_{p}\left(\min\{N^{(1+\alpha-\alpha_{1,i})},T\}\right).

So,
\mathcal{V}_{it}^{-1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\Delta_{k,it}=o_{p}(1)
under our assumptions. Lastly, we can show that

\mathcal{V}_{it}^{-1/2}\left(f_{t}^{0\top}\left(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0}\right)^{-1}\sum_{s=1}^{T}f_{s}^{0}\epsilon_{is}+\lambda_{i}^{0\top}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\right)^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\lambda_{j}^{0}\epsilon_{jt}\right)\to_{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)

by the same assertion as in the proof of Theorem 3 in
Bai, 2003
. It completes the proof.
\square

Proof of Theorems
4.4
.

First, we check the following conditions of Proposition
A.3
:

\kappa q_{t}\to_{p}0,\qquad(\log N)^{\nu/2}\kappa\rho_{2,i}\to_{p}0,\qquad\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{\psi_{\min}}\to_{p}0.

As noted above, we can replace
\psi_{\min}
,
q_{t}
,
\rho_{2,i}
with
N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}
,
T^{-1/2}
,
N^{(\alpha_{2,i}-\alpha-1)/2}
, respectively. In addition, w.h.p.,
\kappa
is bounded. Hence, we have w.h.p.,

\displaystyle\kappa q_{t}\lesssim T^{-1/2}\rightarrow 0,\qquad\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{\psi_{\min}}\asymp\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}}\rightarrow 0,

\displaystyle(\log N)^{\nu/2}\kappa\rho_{2,i}\lesssim(\log N)^{\nu/2}N^{(\alpha_{2,i}-\alpha-1)/2}\leq(\log N)^{\nu/2}N^{-\alpha/2}\rightarrow 0.

Then, the above conditions of Proposition
A.3
are satisfied. In addition, as noted above, we can set
q_{t}
,
\rho_{1,i}
,
\rho_{2,i}
,
\rho_{3,i}
in Assumption E” to
N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}
,
T^{-1/2}
,
N^{(\alpha_{1,i}-\alpha-1)/2}
,
N^{(\alpha_{2,i}-\alpha-1)/2}
,
N^{-\alpha/2}
, respectively. Hence, we can use the bounds of
\left\|\mathcal{R}_{y,i}\right\|
and
\left\|\mathcal{R}_{z,t}\right\|
in Proposition
A.3
by replacing
\psi_{\min}
,
q_{t}
,
\rho_{1,i}
,
\rho_{2,i}
,
\rho_{3,i}
with
N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}
,
T^{-1/2}
,
N^{(\alpha_{1,i}-\alpha-1)/2}
,
N^{(\alpha_{2,i}-\alpha-1)/2}
,
N^{-\alpha/2}
, respectively. Then, by using the similar steps in the Proof of Theorem
3.1
, we can derive the desired results from Proposition
A.3
.
\square

C.4
Proof of Theorems
4.6
and
4.7

The convergence rate and the asymptotic normality of
\widehat{\lambda}_{i}
can be derived by the same token as in the cross-sectional dependence case in Section
C.3
from Proposition
A.4
(i). Similarly, the convergence rate and the asymptotic normality of
\widehat{f}_{t}
can be derived by the same way as in the temporal dependence case in Section
C.2
from Proposition
A.4
(ii). In addition, the bound of
||\widehat{m}_{it}-m^{0}_{it}||
can easily be derived from the bounds of
||\widehat{\lambda}_{i}-\mathbf{H}^{-1}\lambda_{i}^{0}||
and
||\widehat{f}_{t}-\mathbf{H}^{\top}f_{t}^{0}||
. Lastly, for the asymptotic normality of
\widehat{m}_{it}
, the way of proof is basically the same as that of Theorem
3.2
(iii). By the same token as in the proof of Theorem
3.2
(iii) with the aid of Proposition
A.4
, we can derive

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{1,it}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{2,i}-1}{2}}\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}(\log N)^{\omega}}{N^{\alpha}T^{1/2}}+\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{1,i}-1}{2}}\max\{N,T\}}{N^{\alpha}T}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}T^{1/6}}{N^{\alpha/2}T}+\frac{\max\{N^{3/2},T^{3/2}\}(\log N)^{\omega/2}}{N^{3\alpha/2}T^{3/2}}\right)\coloneqq O_{p}(A),

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{2,it}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{1,i}}{2}}\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}(\log N)^{\nu}}{N^{\alpha}T}+\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{1,i}-1}{2}}\max\{N,T\}}{N^{\alpha}T}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\left.+\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{1,i}-1}{2}}\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}N^{1/6}}{N^{\alpha}T^{1/2}}+\frac{N^{\frac{\alpha_{1,i}}{2}}\max\{N^{3/2},T^{3/2}\}(\log N)^{\nu/2}}{N^{2\alpha}T^{3/2}}\right)

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{3,it}\right\|=O_{p}\left(A+\frac{1}{N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}}+\frac{\max\{N,T\}N^{1/6}}{N^{\alpha}T^{3/2}}+\left[\sqrt{\frac{N^{\alpha_{1,i}}}{N}}+\sqrt{\frac{N^{\alpha_{2,i}}}{N}}\right]\frac{\max\{N^{2},T^{2}\}N^{1/6}(\log N)^{\omega}}{N^{2\alpha}T^{2}}\right.

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{\max\{N^{3/2},T^{3/2}\}N^{1/6}}{N^{3\alpha/2}T^{11/6}}+\frac{\max\{N^{5/2},T^{5/2}\}N^{1/6}(\log N)^{\omega/2}}{N^{5\alpha/2}T^{5/2}}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+\frac{\sqrt{N}\max\{N^{3/2},T^{3/2}\}(\log N)^{\nu/2}}{N^{2\alpha}T^{2}}+\left(\sqrt{N^{\alpha_{1,i}}}+\sqrt{N^{\alpha_{2,i}}}\right)\frac{\max\{N^{5/2},T^{5/2}\}(\log N)^{\nu/2+\omega}}{N^{3\alpha}T^{5/2}}

\displaystyle\left.\qquad\quad+\frac{\sqrt{N}\max\{N^{2},T^{2}\}(\log N)^{\nu/2}}{N^{5\alpha/2}T^{7/3}}+\frac{\sqrt{N}\max\{N^{3},T^{3}\}(\log N)^{(\omega+\nu)/2}}{N^{7\alpha/2}T^{3}}\right).

Then,
\mathcal{V}_{it}^{-1/2}\sum_{k=1}^{3}\Delta_{k,it}=o_{p}(1)
where
\mathcal{V}_{it}^{-1}=O_{p}\left(\min\{N^{(1+\alpha-\alpha_{1,i})},T\}\right)
under our assumptions. Lastly, we can show that

\mathcal{V}_{it}^{-1/2}\left(f_{t}^{0\top}\left(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0}\right)^{-1}\sum_{s=1}^{T}f_{s}^{0}\epsilon_{is}+\lambda_{i}^{0\top}\left(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\right)^{-1}\sum_{j=1}^{N}\lambda_{j}^{0}\epsilon_{jt}\right)\to_{d}\mathcal{N}(0,1)

by the same assertion as in the proof of Theorem 3 in
Bai, 2003
. It completes the proof.
\square

C.5
Technical Lemmas

Lemma C.1
.

Let
\mathbf{H}^{0}=\frac{1}{\sqrt{T}}((\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{1/2}\mathbf{G}(\mathbf{D}_{r}^{0})^{-1/2})^{-1}
where
\mathbf{G}
is an eigenvectors matrix of
\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}}{N^{\alpha}}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0}}{T}\right)\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}}{N^{\alpha}}\right)^{1/2}
. Then,
\bm{\Lambda}^{0}=T^{-1/2}\bm{Y}_{r}^{0}(\mathbf{H}^{0})^{-\top}
and
\bm{F}^{0}=T^{1/2}\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0}\mathbf{H}^{0}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.1
.
Let
\bm{\Theta}=\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}}{N^{\alpha}}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0}}{T}\right)\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}}{N^{\alpha}}\right)^{1/2}
and
\mathbf{G}
be a
r\times r
matrix whose columns are the eigenvectors of
\bm{\Theta}
such that
\Delta_{\Theta}=\mathbf{G}^{\top}\bm{\Theta}\mathbf{G}
is a descending order diagonal matrix of the eigenvalues of
\bm{\Theta}
. Define
\mathbf{H}_{\bm{\Lambda}}=(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{-1/2}\mathbf{G}
. Then, we have

\displaystyle(\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0}\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top})\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\mathbf{H}_{\bm{\Lambda}}

\displaystyle=\bm{\Lambda}^{0}(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{-1/2}(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{1/2}\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0}(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{1/2}(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{1/2}\mathbf{H}_{\bm{\Lambda}}

\displaystyle=\bm{\Lambda}^{0}(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{-1/2}\left[(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{1/2}\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0}(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{1/2}\mathbf{G}\right]

\displaystyle=\bm{\Lambda}^{0}(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{-1/2}(N^{\alpha}T)\bm{\Theta}\mathbf{G}

\displaystyle=\bm{\Lambda}^{0}(\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0})^{-1/2}\mathbf{G}(N^{\alpha}T)\Delta_{\Theta}

\displaystyle=\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\mathbf{H}_{\bm{\Lambda}}(N^{\alpha}T)\Delta_{\Theta}.

In addition, note that
(\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\mathbf{H}_{\bm{\Lambda}})^{\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\mathbf{H}_{\bm{\Lambda}}=\mathbf{H}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\mathbf{H}_{\bm{\Lambda}}=\mathbf{G}^{\top}\mathbf{G}=I_{r}
. Therefore, the column of
\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\mathbf{H}_{\bm{\Lambda}}
are the eigenvectors of
\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0}\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}
and the left singular vectors of
\bm{M}^{0}=\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\bm{F}^{0\top}
. So,
\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0}=\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\mathbf{H}_{\bm{\Lambda}}
and
\bm{Y}_{r}^{0}=\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\mathbf{H}_{\bm{\Lambda}}(\mathbf{D}_{r}^{0})^{1/2}=\sqrt{T}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}(\mathbf{H}^{0})^{\top}
. Then,
\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0}=T^{-1/2}\bm{F}^{0}(\mathbf{H}^{0})^{-1}
follows from
\bm{Y}_{r}^{0}=\sqrt{T}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}(\mathbf{H}^{0})^{\top}
.
\square

Lemma C.2
.

(i)
\mathbf{G}\to_{p}\mathcal{G}^{*}
where
\mathcal{G}^{*}
is the eigenvector of
\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}
corresponding to the sign of
\mathbf{G}
.

(ii)
\frac{1}{N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}}\mathbf{D}_{r}^{0}\to_{p}\mathcal{D}
and
\frac{1}{N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}}\mathbf{D}_{r}\to_{p}\mathcal{D}
.

(iii)
(N^{\alpha/4}T^{1/4})\mathbf{H}^{0}\to_{p}\mathcal{D}^{1/2}\mathcal{G}^{*\top}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{-1/2}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.2
.

(i) Since
\bm{\Theta}
converges to
\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}
by Assumption A, the eigenvalues of
\bm{\Theta}
will be distinct for large
N
and
T
. So, the eigenvector matrix of
\bm{\Theta}
is unique except that each column can be replaced by the negative of itself. As we can know in the proof of Lemma
C.1
, the sign of
\mathbf{G}
is determined by the sign alignment between
\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0}
and
\bm{\Lambda}^{0}
. Then, by the eigenvector perturbation theory, there is a unique eigenvector of
\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{F}}\bm{\Sigma}_{\bm{\Lambda}}^{1/2}
, says,
\mathcal{G}^{*}
, such that
||\mathbf{G}-\mathcal{G}^{*}||=o_{p}(1)
and the sign of
\mathcal{G}^{*}
corresponds to that of
\mathbf{G}
.

(ii) Note that the square roots of eigenvalues of
\bm{\Theta}
are the same as the singular values of
\frac{1}{N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\bm{F}^{0\top}
, that is,
\frac{1}{N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}}\mathbf{D}_{r}^{0}
. Then, by the continuity of eigenvalues, we have
\frac{1}{N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}}\mathbf{D}_{r}^{0}\to_{p}\mathcal{D}
. In addition, note that

\frac{1}{N^{\alpha}T}\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}=\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{0}(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0})\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}}{N^{\alpha}T}+\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{0}\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{E}^{\top}}{N^{\alpha}T}+\frac{\bm{E}\bm{F}^{0}\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}}{N^{\alpha}T}+\frac{\bm{E}\bm{E}^{\top}}{N^{\alpha}T}=\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{0}(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0})\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}}{N^{\alpha}T}+o_{p}(1)

since
||\bm{E}||=O_{p}(\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\})
,
||\bm{\Lambda}^{0}||=O_{p}(N^{\alpha/2})
, and
||\bm{F}^{0}||=O_{p}(T^{1/2})
. By the matrix perturbation theorem, the
r
largest eigenvalues of
\frac{1}{N^{\alpha}T}\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}
, that is,
\frac{1}{N^{\alpha}T}\mathbf{D}_{r}^{2}
, are determined by
\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{0}(\bm{F}^{0\top}\bm{F}^{0})\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}}{N^{\alpha}T}
whose eigenvalues are
\frac{1}{N^{\alpha}T}(\mathbf{D}_{r}^{0})^{2}
. Hence,
\frac{1}{N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}}\mathbf{D}_{r}\to_{p}\mathcal{D}
.

(iii) Note that

(N^{\alpha/4}T^{1/4})\mathbf{H}^{0}=\left(\frac{\mathbf{D}_{r}^{0}}{N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}}\right)^{1/2}\mathbf{G}^{\top}\left(\frac{\bm{\Lambda}^{0\top}\bm{\Lambda}^{0}}{N^{\alpha}}\right)^{-1/2}.

Hence, the result follows from Assumption A, Lemmas
C.2
(i), and (ii).
\square

Lemma C.3
.

\mathbf{O}\to_{p}\mathcal{I}_{sgn}
where
\mathcal{I}_{sgn}
is a
r\times r
diagonal matrix consisting of the diagonal elements of
\pm 1
and the signs are determined by the sign alignment between
\mathbf{U}_{r}
and
\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0}
.

Proof of Lemma
C.3
.

First, we derive the limit of
\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}
. For simplicity, let
K_{NT}=N^{\alpha/2}T^{1/2}
. From the relations
\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}=\mathbf{U}_{r}\mathbf{D}_{r}^{2}
and
\bm{X}=\bm{Y}_{r}^{0}\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0\top}+\bm{E}
, we have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0}}{K_{NT}}\right)^{1/2}\frac{\bm{Y}_{r}^{0\top}}{\sqrt{K_{NT}}}\frac{\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}}{K_{NT}^{2}}\mathbf{U}_{r}=\left(\frac{\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0}}{K_{NT}}\right)^{1/2}\frac{\bm{Y}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}}{\sqrt{K_{NT}}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{D}_{r}^{2}}{K_{NT}^{2}}\right),

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0}}{K_{NT}}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{\bm{Y}_{r}^{0\top}\bm{Y}_{r}^{0}}{K_{NT}}\right)\left(\frac{\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0}}{K_{NT}}\right)\left(\frac{\bm{Y}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}}{\sqrt{K_{NT}}}\right)+d_{NT}=\left(\frac{\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0}}{K_{NT}}\right)^{1/2}\frac{\bm{Y}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}}{\sqrt{K_{NT}}}\left(\frac{\mathbf{D}_{r}^{2}}{K_{NT}^{2}}\right)

where

d_{NT}=\left(\frac{\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0}}{K_{NT}}\right)^{1/2}\left[\frac{\bm{Y}_{r}^{0\top}\bm{Y}_{r}^{0}}{K_{NT}}\frac{\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0\top}}{\sqrt{K_{NT}}}\frac{\bm{E}^{\top}}{K_{NT}}+\frac{\bm{Y}_{r}^{0\top}}{\sqrt{K_{NT}}}\frac{\bm{E}}{K_{NT}}\frac{\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0}\bm{Y}_{r}^{0\top}}{K_{NT}}+\frac{\bm{Y}_{r}^{0\top}}{\sqrt{K_{NT}}}\frac{\bm{E}}{K_{NT}}\frac{\bm{E}^{\top}}{K_{NT}}\right]\mathbf{U}_{r}.

Because
\left\|\frac{\bm{Y}_{r}^{0\top}\bm{Y}_{r}^{0}}{\psi_{\min}}\right\|,\left\|\frac{\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0}}{\psi_{\min}}\right\|=O_{p}(\kappa)
,
\left\|\frac{\bm{Y}_{r}^{0}}{\sqrt{\psi_{\min}}}\right\|,\left\|\frac{\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0}}{\sqrt{\psi_{\min}}}\right\|=O_{p}(\kappa^{1/2})
,
\left\|\frac{\bm{E}}{\psi_{\min}}\right\|=O_{p}\left(\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{\psi_{\min}}\right)
,
\psi_{\min}\asymp K_{NT}
, and
\kappa
is bounded under our assumptions, we have
d_{NT}=o_{p}(1)
. Additionally, define

\displaystyle B_{NT}=\left(\frac{\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0}}{K_{NT}}\right)^{1/2}\left(\frac{\bm{Y}_{r}^{0\top}\bm{Y}_{r}^{0}}{K_{NT}}\right)\left(\frac{\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0}}{K_{NT}}\right)^{1/2}=\left(\frac{\mathbf{D}_{r}^{0}}{K_{NT}}\right)^{2},

\displaystyle R_{NT}=\left(\frac{\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0}}{K_{NT}}\right)^{1/2}\frac{\bm{Y}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}}{\sqrt{K_{NT}}}=\frac{\mathbf{D}_{r}^{0}}{K_{NT}}\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{U}_{r},

\displaystyle V_{NT}=\frac{\mathbf{D}_{r}}{K_{NT}}.

Then, since
R_{NT}
is invertible by Claim
3
, we have

[B_{NT}+d_{NT}R_{NT}^{-1}]R_{NT}=R_{NT}V_{NT}^{2}.

Claim 3
.

\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}
is invertible and
||(\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{U}_{r})^{-1}||
is bounded with probability converging to 1.

In addition, to normalize
R_{NT}
, define
\bar{R}_{NT}=R_{NT}V_{NT}^{*-1}
where
V_{NT}^{*2}
is a diagonal matrix consisting of the diagonal elements of
R_{NT}^{\top}R_{NT}=(\mathbf{U}_{r}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0})\left(\frac{\mathbf{D}_{r}^{0}}{K_{NT}}\right)^{2}(\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{U}_{r})
. Then, we have

[B_{NT}+d_{NT}R_{NT}^{-1}]\bar{R}_{NT}=\bar{R}_{NT}V_{NT}^{2}

and
\bar{R}_{NT}
is the eigenvector of
B_{NT}+d_{NT}R_{NT}^{-1}
. Because
B_{NT}\to_{p}\mathcal{D}^{2}
by Lemma
C.2
(ii) and
d_{NT}R_{NT}^{-1}=o_{p}(1)
,
B_{NT}+d_{NT}R_{NT}^{-1}\to_{p}\mathcal{D}^{2}
. Since the eigenvalues of
\mathcal{D}^{2}
are distinct, those of
B_{NT}+d_{NT}R_{NT}^{-1}
will be distinct for large
N
and
T
. So, the eigenvector of
B_{NT}+d_{NT}R_{NT}^{-1}
is unique up to the column sign, and the sign of
\bar{R}_{NT}
is determined by the sign alignment between
\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0}
and
\mathbf{U}_{r}
. By the eigenvector perturbation theory, there is a unique eigenvector of
\mathcal{D}^{2}
, says,
\mathcal{I}_{sgn}
, such that
||\bar{R}_{NT}-\mathcal{I}_{sgn}||=o_{p}(1)
and the sign of
\mathcal{I}_{sgn}
corresponds to that of
\bar{R}_{NT}
. In addition, since
\mathcal{I}_{sgn}
is the eigenvector of the diagonal matrix
\mathcal{D}^{2}
, it is a
r\times r
diagonal matrix consisting of the diagonal elements of
\pm 1
.

Note that
\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}=\left(\frac{\mathbf{D}_{r}^{0}}{K_{NT}}\right)^{-1}\bar{R}_{NT}V^{*}_{NT}
. Then, By Lemma
C.2
(ii) and Claim
4
with the above result, we have

\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}\to_{p}\mathcal{D}^{-1}\mathcal{I}_{sgn}\mathcal{D}=\mathcal{I}_{sgn}.

Here,
\mathcal{I}_{sgn}
is the
r\times r
diagonal matrix consisting of the diagonal elements of
\pm 1
and the sign of these are determined by the sign alignment between
\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0}
and
\mathbf{U}_{r}
.

Claim 4
.

R_{NT}^{\top}R_{NT}=(\mathbf{U}_{r}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0})\left(\frac{\mathbf{D}_{r}^{0}}{K_{NT}}\right)^{2}(\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{U}_{r})\to_{p}\mathcal{D}^{2}
.

Lastly, we show that
\mathbf{O}\to_{p}\mathcal{I}_{sgn}
. Let
\mathbf{Q}=\argmin_{\mathbf{R}\in\mathcal{O}^{r\times r}}\left\|\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{U}_{r}\\
\mathbf{V}_{r}\end{bmatrix}\mathbf{R}-\begin{bmatrix}\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0}\\
\mathbf{V}_{r}^{0}\end{bmatrix}\right\|_{F}
. Then, as noted in the proof of Lemma
B.3
, by Lemma B.4 of
Chen et al., 2020a
,
||\mathbf{O}-\mathbf{Q}||=o_{p}(1)
. In addition, we have

\mathbf{U}_{r}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0}=\mathbf{U}_{r}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}\mathbf{Q}+\mathbf{U}_{r}^{\top}(\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0}-\mathbf{U}_{r}\mathbf{Q})=\mathbf{Q}+\mathbf{U}_{r}^{\top}(\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0}-\mathbf{U}_{r}\mathbf{Q}).

As noted in the proof of Lemma
B.3
, by Lemma B.2 of
Chen et al., 2020a
, we have
||\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0}-\mathbf{U}_{r}\mathbf{Q}||=O_{p}\left(\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{K_{NT}}\right)=o_{p}(1)
. Hence, we have

\left\|\mathbf{U}_{r}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0}-\mathbf{Q}\right\|\leq\left\|\mathbf{U}_{r}\right\|\left\|\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0}-\mathbf{U}_{r}\mathbf{Q}\right\|=o_{p}(1).

Then, because
\left\|\mathbf{O}-\mathbf{U}_{r}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0}\right\|=o_{p}(1)
, we have
\mathbf{O}\to_{p}\mathcal{I}_{sgn}
.
\square

Proof of Claim
3
.

As noted above, by Lemma B.2 of
Chen et al., 2020a
, we have
||\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0}-\mathbf{U}_{r}\mathbf{Q}||=O_{p}\left(\frac{\max\{\sqrt{N},\sqrt{T}\}}{K_{NT}}\right)=o_{p}(1)
. Hence, by Weyl’s inequality, we have w.h.p., that

\psi_{r}(\mathbf{U}_{r}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0})\geq\psi_{r}(\mathbf{Q})-\left\|\mathbf{U}_{r}^{\top}(\mathbf{U}_{r}^{0}-\mathbf{U}_{r}\mathbf{Q})\right\|\geq\frac{1}{2}.\ \ \square

Proof of Claim
4
.

We have by Lemma
C.2
(ii) that

\frac{\mathbf{U}_{r}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}}{K_{NT}^{2}}=\frac{\mathbf{D}_{r}^{2}}{K_{NT}^{2}}\to_{p}\mathcal{D}^{2}.

In addition, by the same token as in the proof of Lemma
C.2
(ii), we can show that

R_{NT}^{\top}R_{NT}=\frac{\mathbf{U}_{r}^{\top}\bm{Y}_{r}^{0}\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{Z}_{r}^{0}\bm{Y}_{r}^{0\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}}{K_{NT}^{2}}=\frac{\mathbf{U}_{r}^{\top}\bm{X}\bm{X}^{\top}\mathbf{U}_{r}}{K_{NT}^{2}}+o_{p}(1).

Hence,
R_{NT}^{\top}R_{NT}\to_{p}\mathcal{D}^{2}
.
\square

C.6
Proof of Lemma
4.1

Proof of Lemma
4.1
(i).

Let
\epsilon_{it}
be a MA(q) process with
q\leq C_{1}(\log N)^{\nu}
for some constant
C_{1}>0
. Then, we set
\delta=C_{1}\lceil(\ln N)^{\nu}\rceil
. Let
a\geq 1
be a natural number. Note that
\epsilon_{it}
is a function of
(u_{i,t-q},\dots,u_{it})
and
\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+a}
is a function of
(u_{i,t+\delta+a-q},\dots,u_{i,t+\delta+a})
. Because
\delta-q\geq 0
, there is no intersection between
(u_{i,t-q},\dots,u_{it})
and
(u_{i,t+\delta+a-q},\dots,u_{i,t+\delta+a})
. Hence,
\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+a}
is independent of
\epsilon_{it}
. Similarly,
\epsilon_{i,t-\delta-a}
is independent of
\epsilon_{it}
. Hence,
(\epsilon_{is})_{s\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)^{c}}
is independent of
\epsilon_{it}
and
\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{it}|(\epsilon_{is})_{s\in\mathcal{N}_{\delta}(t)^{c}}]=\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{it}]
.
\square

Proof of Lemma
4.1
(ii).

It is instructive to start with the case of AR(1) process.

Lemma C.4
.

For each
i\in[N]
, let
\epsilon_{it}
be a stationary AR(1) process such that

\epsilon_{it}=\phi_{(i)}\epsilon_{i,t-1}+u_{it},\ \ \text{ where }\max_{i}\left|\phi_{(i)}\right|<\vartheta<1,\ \ u_{it}\sim i.i.d.\ \ \mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{u,i}^{2}).

Define
\epsilon_{i,pre}=(\epsilon_{i1},\dots,\epsilon_{i,t-\delta-1})
and
\epsilon_{i,post}=(\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{iT})
. If
\delta=C\lceil\ln N\rceil
for some constant
C>0
, we have

\max_{i}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{it}|\epsilon_{i,pre},\epsilon_{i,post}\right]^{2}\right]\lesssim N^{-1}.

Proof of Lemma
C.4
.

Step 1.
First, we show that for each
i
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{it}|\epsilon_{i,pre},\epsilon_{i,post}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{it}|\epsilon_{i,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+1}\right].

Fix
i=i_{o}
. For notational simplicity, let
\epsilon_{i_{o},t}=\epsilon_{o,t}
,
\epsilon_{i_{o},pre}=\epsilon_{o,pre}
,
\epsilon_{i_{o},post}=\epsilon_{o,post}
,
u_{i_{o},t}=u_{o,t}
, and
\phi_{(i_{o})}=\phi_{o}
. Because, for all
s\geq 1
,

\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1+s}=\phi_{o}^{s}\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}+\sum_{k=0}^{s-1}\phi_{o}^{k}u_{o,t+\delta+1+s-k},

conditioning on
\{\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\epsilon_{o,pre}\}
,
(\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1+s})_{s\geq 1}
is independent of
\epsilon_{o,t}
. Hence, we can say

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,post}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+2},\dots,\epsilon_{o,T}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}\right].

(13)

In addition, because
\epsilon_{o,t}=\phi_{o}^{\delta+1}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1}+\sum_{k=0}^{\delta}\phi_{o}^{k}u_{o,t-k}
, we have by Claim
5
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\phi_{o}^{\delta+1}\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}\right]+\sum_{k=0}^{\delta}\phi_{o}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,t-k}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\phi_{o}^{\delta+1}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1}+\sum_{k=0}^{\delta}\phi_{o}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,t-k}|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}\right].

Claim 5
.

For
t-\delta\leq s\leq t
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}\right]
.

Hence, we know

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}\right]|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\phi_{o}^{\delta+1}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1}+\sum_{k=0}^{\delta}\phi_{o}^{k}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,t-k}|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}\right],

and so,
\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}\right]
. Then, with (
13
), it shows that

\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,post}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}\right].

Therefore, for any
i
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{it}|\epsilon_{i,pre},\epsilon_{i,post}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{it}|\epsilon_{i,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+1}\right].

Proof of Claim
5
.
Note that

\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}=\phi_{o}^{(2\delta+2)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1}+Q,\ \ \text{ where }Q=\sum_{k=0}^{2\delta+1}\phi_{o}^{k}u_{o,t+\delta+1-k}.

Because
Q=\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}-\phi_{o}^{(2\delta+2)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1}
and
\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}=Q+\phi_{o}^{(2\delta+2)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1}
, there are continuous functions
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
such that
h_{1}(\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\epsilon_{o,pre})=(Q,\epsilon_{o,pre})
and
h_{2}(Q,\epsilon_{o,pre})=(\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\epsilon_{o,pre})
. Hence, the sigma-algebra generated by
(Q,\epsilon_{o,pre})
is the same as that by
(\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\epsilon_{o,pre})
. So, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\epsilon_{o,pre}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|Q,\epsilon_{o,pre}\right].

In addition, because
\epsilon_{o,pre}
is a function of
\{u_{o,k}:k\leq t-\delta-1\}
while
Q
is a function of
\{u_{o,k}:t-\delta\leq k\leq t+\delta+1\}
,
\epsilon_{o,pre}
is independent of
Q
and
u_{o,s}
where
t-\delta\leq s\leq t
. So, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|Q,\epsilon_{o,pre}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|Q\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}-\phi_{o}^{(2\delta+2)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1}\right]=f(\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1})

for some function
f
. Then, since

\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\epsilon_{o,pre}\right]|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}\right]=f(\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}),

we have
\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}\right]
.
\square

Step 2.
Because
(u_{is})_{s\leq T}
is a multivariate normal,
(\epsilon_{i,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+1},\epsilon_{it})
has the following multivariate normal distribution:

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\epsilon_{i,t-\delta-1}\\
\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+1}\\
\epsilon_{it}\end{pmatrix}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\begin{pmatrix}0\\
0\\
0\end{pmatrix},\frac{\sigma_{u,i}^{2}}{1-\phi_{(i)}^{2}}\begin{pmatrix}1&\phi_{(i)}^{2\delta+2}&\phi_{(i)}^{\delta+1}\\
\phi_{(i)}^{2\delta+2}&1&\phi_{(i)}^{\delta+1}\\
\phi_{(i)}^{\delta+1}&\phi_{(i)}^{\delta+1}&1\end{pmatrix}\right).

Here, the variance form comes from the auto-covariance of the stationary AR(1) process. Then, since the conditional expectation of the multivariate normal distribution has a linear form, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{it}|\epsilon_{i,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+1}\right]

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\phi_{(i)}^{\delta+1}&\phi_{(i)}^{\delta+1}\end{pmatrix}\begin{pmatrix}1&\phi_{(i)}^{2\delta+2}\\
\phi_{(i)}^{2\delta+2}&1\end{pmatrix}^{-1}\begin{pmatrix}\epsilon_{t-\delta-1}\\
\epsilon_{t+\delta+1}\end{pmatrix}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{1-\phi_{(i)}^{4\delta+4}}\left(\phi_{(i)}^{\delta+1}-\phi_{(i)}^{3\delta+3}\right)\left(\epsilon_{t-\delta-1}+\epsilon_{t+\delta+1}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{\phi_{(i)}^{\delta+1}}{1+\phi_{(i)}^{2\delta+2}}\left(\epsilon_{t-\delta-1}+\epsilon_{t+\delta+1}\right).

Hence, we have for all
i
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{it}|\epsilon_{i,pre},\epsilon_{i,post}\right]^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{it}|\epsilon_{i,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+1}\right]^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=2\sigma_{u,i}^{2}\left(\frac{\phi_{(i)}^{\delta+1}}{1+\phi_{(i)}^{2\delta+2}}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{1}{1-\phi_{(i)}^{2}}+\frac{\phi_{(i)}^{\delta+1}}{1-\phi_{(i)}^{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\phi_{(i)}^{\delta+1}}{1+\phi_{(i)}^{2\delta+2}}\right)^{2}\lesssim\phi_{(i)}^{2\delta}\lesssim\vartheta^{2\delta}.

Note that
\delta=C\lceil\ln N\rceil\geq C\ln N
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\vartheta^{2\delta}=e^{2\delta\ln\vartheta}\leq e^{2C\ln N\ln\vartheta}=\exp\left(\ln N^{(2C\ln\vartheta)}\right)=N^{(2C\ln\vartheta)}.

In addition, if
C\geq\frac{1}{2\ln(\vartheta^{-1})}
, we have
2C\ln\vartheta\leq-1
. Hence, when
C\geq\frac{1}{2\ln(\vartheta^{-1})}
, we have

\max_{i}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{it}|\epsilon_{i,pre},\epsilon_{i,post}\right]^{2}\right]\lesssim N^{-1}.\ \ \square

Then, we can generalize the above result to the case of AR(p) process.

Lemma C.5
.

For each
i\in[N]
,
\epsilon_{it}
is a stationary AR(p) process such that

\epsilon_{it}=\phi^{(i)}_{1}\epsilon_{i,t-1}+\cdots+\phi^{(i)}_{p}\epsilon_{i,t-p}+u_{it},\ \ \text{ where }u_{it}\sim i.i.d.\ \ \mathcal{N}(0,\sigma_{u,i}^{2}),

and there is a constant
0<\vartheta<1
such that
\max_{1\leq i\leq N,1\leq k\leq p}\left|\psi^{(i)}_{k}\right|<\vartheta
, where
(\psi^{(i)}_{1},\dots,\psi^{(i)}_{p})
are the roots of the characteristic polynomial

\psi^{p}-\phi_{1}^{(i)}\psi^{p-1}-\cdots-\phi^{(i)}_{p-1}\psi-\phi^{(i)}_{p}=0.

Then, if
\delta=C\lceil\ln N\rceil
for some large constant
C>0
, we have for sufficiently large
\delta
,

\max_{i}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{it}|\epsilon_{i,pre},\epsilon_{i,post}\right]^{2}\right]\leq CN^{-1}

for some constant
C>0
.

Proof of Lemma
C.5
.

Step 1.
First, we show that for each
i
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{it}|\epsilon_{i,pre},\epsilon_{i,post}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{it}|\epsilon_{i,t-\delta-p},\dots,\epsilon_{i,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+p}\right].

Fix
i=i_{o}
. For notational simplicity, let
\epsilon_{i_{o},t}=\epsilon_{o,t}
,
\epsilon_{i_{o},pre}=\epsilon_{o,pre}
,
\epsilon_{i_{o},post}=\epsilon_{o,post}
,
u_{i_{o},t}=u_{o,t}
, and
\phi^{(i_{o})}_{k}=\phi_{o,k}
where
1\leq k\leq p
. Note that, for all
s\geq 1
, there are constants
a_{1,s},\cdots,a_{p,s},b_{0},\dots,b_{s-1}
depending on
\{\phi_{o,1},\dots,\phi_{o,p}\}
such that

\epsilon_{o,t}=a_{1,s}\epsilon_{o,t-s}+\cdots+a_{p,s}\epsilon_{o,t-s-p+1}+\sum_{k=0}^{s-1}b_{k}u_{o,t-k}.

Then, because we have for all
s\geq 1
,

\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p+s}=a_{1,s}\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}+\cdots+a_{p,s}\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}+\sum_{k=0}^{s-1}b_{k}u_{o,t+\delta+p+s-k},

conditioning on
\{\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p},\epsilon_{o,pre}\}
,
(\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p+s})_{s\geq 1}
is independent of
\epsilon_{o,t}
. Hence, we can say

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,post}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+2},\dots,\epsilon_{o,T}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}\right].

(14)

In addition, because

\epsilon_{o,t}=a_{1,(\delta+1)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1}+\cdots+a_{p,(\delta+1)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p}+\sum_{k=0}^{\delta}b_{k}u_{o,t-k},

we have by Claim
6
that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[a_{1,(\delta+1)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1}+\cdots+a_{p,(\delta+1)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}\right]

\displaystyle\ \ +\sum_{k=0}^{\delta}b_{k}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,t-k}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}\right]

\displaystyle=a_{1,(\delta+1)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1}+\cdots+a_{p,(\delta+1)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p}

\displaystyle\ \ +\sum_{k=0}^{\delta}b_{k}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,t-k}|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}\right].

Claim 6
.

For
t-\delta\leq s\leq t
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}\right].

Hence, we know

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}\right]|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}\right]

\displaystyle=a_{1,(\delta+1)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1}+\cdots+a_{p,(\delta+1)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p}+\sum_{k=0}^{\delta}b_{k}\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,t-k}|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}\right],

and so,

\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+p}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}\right].

Then, with (
14
), it shows that

\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,post}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{o,t}|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}\right].

Therefore, for any
i
,

\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{it}|\epsilon_{i,pre},\epsilon_{i,post}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{it}|\epsilon_{i,t-\delta-p},\dots,\epsilon_{i,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+p}\right].

Proof of Claim
6
.
Note that

\displaystyle\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1}=a_{1,(2\delta+2)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1}+\cdots+a_{p,(2\delta+2)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p}+Q_{1},\ \ \text{ where }Q_{1}=\sum_{k=0}^{2\delta+1}b_{k}u_{o,t+\delta+1-k},

\displaystyle\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+2}=a_{1,(2\delta+3)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1}+\cdots+a_{p,(2\delta+3)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p}+Q_{2},\ \ \text{ where }Q_{2}=\sum_{k=0}^{2\delta+2}b_{k}u_{o,t+\delta+2-k},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\vdots

\displaystyle\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}=a_{1,(2\delta+p+1)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1}+\cdots+a_{p,(2\delta+p+1)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p}+Q_{p},\ \ \text{ where }Q_{p}=\sum_{k=0}^{2\delta+p}b_{k}u_{o,t+\delta+p-k}.

Because for all
1\leq l\leq p
,

\displaystyle Q_{l}=\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+l}-(a_{1,(2\delta+l+1)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1}+\cdots+a_{l,(2\delta+l+1)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p}),

\displaystyle\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+l}=Q_{l}+a_{1,(2\delta+l+1)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1}+\cdots+a_{l,(2\delta+l+1)}\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p},

there are continuous functions
h_{1}
and
h_{2}
such that
h_{1}(\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p},\epsilon_{o,pre})=(Q_{1},\dots,Q_{p},\epsilon_{o,pre})
and
h_{2}(Q_{1},\dots,Q_{p},\epsilon_{o,pre})=(\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p},\epsilon_{o,pre})
. Hence, the sigma-algebra generated by
(Q_{1},\dots,Q_{p},\epsilon_{o,pre})
is the same as that by
(\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p},\epsilon_{o,pre})
. So, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p},\epsilon_{o,pre}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|Q_{1},\dots,Q_{p},\epsilon_{o,pre}\right].

In addition, because
\epsilon_{o,pre}
is a function of
\{u_{o,k}:k\leq t-\delta-1\}
while
Q_{l}
is a function of
\{u_{o,k}:t-\delta\leq k\leq t+\delta+l\}
,
\epsilon_{o,pre}
is independent of
(Q_{1},\dots,Q_{p})
and
u_{o,s}
where
t-\delta\leq s\leq t
. So, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|Q_{1},\dots,Q_{p},\epsilon_{o,pre}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|Q_{1},\dots,Q_{p}\right]=f(\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p})

for some function
f
, since
Q_{l}
is a function of
(\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+l})
. Then, because

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}\right]|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}\right]

\displaystyle=f(\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}),

we have
\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|\epsilon_{o,pre},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[u_{o,s}|\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-p},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{o,t+\delta+p}\right]
.
\square

Step 2.
Let
\mathcal{E}^{(i)}_{x}=[\epsilon_{i,t-\delta-1},\dots,\epsilon_{i,t-\delta-p},\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+p}]^{\top}
and
\mathcal{E}^{(i)}_{y}=[\epsilon_{it}]
. Because
(u_{is})_{s\leq T}
is a multivariate normal,
(\mathcal{E}^{(i)}_{x},\mathcal{E}^{(i)}_{y})
has the following multivariate normal distribution:

\displaystyle\begin{pmatrix}\mathcal{E}_{x}^{(i)}\\
\mathcal{E}_{y}^{(i)}\end{pmatrix}\sim\mathcal{N}\left(\begin{pmatrix}0\\
0\end{pmatrix},\begin{pmatrix}\Sigma_{xx}^{(i)}&\Sigma_{xy}^{(i)}\\
\Sigma_{yx}^{(i)}&\Sigma_{yy}^{(i)}\end{pmatrix}\right),\quad\text{where}

\displaystyle\underbrace{\Sigma_{xx}^{(i)}}_{2p\times 2p}=\begin{pmatrix}\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)}&\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\\
\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}&\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)}\end{pmatrix},\ \ \underbrace{\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)}}_{p\times p}=\begin{pmatrix}\gamma^{(i)}_{0}&\cdots&\gamma^{(i)}_{p-1}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\gamma^{(i)}_{p-1}&\cdots&\gamma^{(i)}_{0}\end{pmatrix},\ \ \underbrace{\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}}_{p\times p}=\begin{pmatrix}\gamma^{(i)}_{(2\delta+2)}&\cdots&\gamma^{(i)}_{(2\delta+p+1)}\\
\vdots&\ddots&\vdots\\
\gamma^{(i)}_{(2\delta+p+1)}&\cdots&\gamma^{(i)}_{(2\delta+2p)}\end{pmatrix},

\displaystyle\underbrace{\Sigma_{yx}^{(i)}}_{1\times 2p}=\begin{pmatrix}\gamma^{(i)}_{\delta+1}&\cdots&\gamma^{(i)}_{\delta+p}&\gamma^{(i)}_{\delta+1}&\cdots&\gamma^{(i)}_{\delta+p}\end{pmatrix},\ \ \Sigma_{xy}^{(i)}=\Sigma_{yx}^{(i)\top},\ \ \Sigma_{yy}^{(i)}=\gamma^{(i)}_{0}.

Here,
\gamma^{(i)}_{s}
is the auto-covariance such that
\gamma^{(i)}_{s}=\mathbb{E}[\epsilon_{it}\epsilon_{i,t-s}]
. Then, since the conditional expectation of the multivariate normal distribution has a linear form, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{it}|\epsilon_{i,t-\delta-p},\dots,\epsilon_{i,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+p}\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathcal{E}^{(i)}_{y}|\mathcal{E}^{(i)}_{x}\right]=\Sigma^{(i)}_{yx}\Sigma_{xx}^{(i)-1}\mathcal{E}^{(i)}_{x}.

To derive the bound of
\left\|\Sigma_{xx}^{(i)-1}\right\|
, we first get the bound of
\left\|\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}\right\|
. Since
\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)}
is an auto-covariance matrix, by Proposition 1 of
Lin and Ho, 2008
, there is a constant
C_{loc}>0
such that, for all
i
,
\left\|\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}\right\|\leq C_{loc}\frac{1}{\sigma_{u,i}^{2}}
. In addition, since
p
is finite, we have

\left\|\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\right\|\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\left|\gamma^{(i)}_{(2\delta+2)}\right|
. Then, by Claim
7
, there is a constant
C>0
such that

\max_{i}\left\|\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}\right\|^{2}\leq C\frac{\vartheta^{(4\delta+4)}}{(1-\vartheta^{2})^{2}}.

Note that, because
\vartheta<1
, there is
\delta_{o}>0
such that for all
\delta>\delta_{o}
, we have
\max_{i}\left\|\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}\right\|^{2}\leq 1/2
. Then, when
\delta>\delta_{o}
, we have for all
i
,

\left\|\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\right\|\leq\left\|\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}\right\|\leq\frac{1}{2\left\|\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}\right\|}=\frac{1}{2}\psi_{p}\left(\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)}\right),

and so, by Weyl’s theorem, when
\delta>\delta_{o}
, we have for all
i
that

\displaystyle\psi_{p}\left(\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)}-\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\right)\geq\psi_{p}\left(\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)}\right)-\left\|\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\right\|\geq\frac{1}{2}\psi_{p}\left(\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)}\right)\geq\frac{1}{2C_{loc}}\sigma_{u,i}^{2}.

Hence, when
\delta>\delta_{o}
, we have for all
i
,
\left\|\left(\Sigma^{(i)}_{loc}-\Sigma^{(i)}_{int}\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}\Sigma^{(i)}_{int}\right)^{-1}\right\|\leq\frac{2C_{loc}}{\sigma_{u,i}^{2}}
. Moreover, since
\Sigma_{xx}^{(i)-1}
consists of the block matrices
\left(\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)}-\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\right)^{-1}
and
\left(\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)}-\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\right)^{-1}\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}
, when
\delta>\delta_{o}
, we have for all
i
,

\displaystyle\left\|\Sigma_{xx}^{(i)-1}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|\left(\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)}-\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\right)^{-1}\right\|+2\left\|\left(\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)}-\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\right)^{-1}\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|\left(\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)}-\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\right)^{-1}\right\|\left(1+\left\|\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\right\|\left\|\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}\right\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2\left\|\left(\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)}-\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\Sigma_{loc}^{(i)-1}\Sigma_{int}^{(i)}\right)^{-1}\right\|\left(1+\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8C_{loc}}{\sigma_{u,i}^{2}}.

In addition, since
p
is finite, we have

\left\|\Sigma_{yx}^{(i)}\right\|\lesssim\left\|\Sigma_{yx}^{(i)}\right\|_{\infty}\lesssim\left|\gamma^{(i)}_{\delta+1}\right|
. Therefore, we have by Claim
7
that, whenever
\delta>\delta_{o}
,

\displaystyle\max_{i}\mathbb{E}[\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{it}|\epsilon_{i,t-\delta-p},\dots,\epsilon_{i,t-\delta-1},\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+1},\dots,\epsilon_{i,t+\delta+p}\right]^{2}]

\displaystyle=\max_{i}\Sigma_{yx}^{(i)}\Sigma_{xx}^{(i)-1}\mathbb{E}[\mathcal{E}_{x}^{(i)}\mathcal{E}_{x}^{(i)\top}]\Sigma_{xx}^{(i)-1}\Sigma_{yx}^{(i)\top}

\displaystyle\leq\max_{i}\left\|\Sigma_{xx}^{(i)-1}\right\|^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{yx}^{(i)}\right\|^{2}\left\|\Sigma_{xx}^{(i)}\right\|

\displaystyle\leq C_{1}\max_{i}\sigma_{u,i}^{2}\frac{\vartheta^{2\delta+2}}{(1-\vartheta^{2})^{2}}\leq C_{2}\vartheta^{2\delta},

for some constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
. Then, because
\delta=C\lceil\ln N\rceil\geq C\ln N
, we have

\displaystyle\vartheta^{2\delta}=e^{2\delta\ln\vartheta}\leq e^{2C\ln N\ln\vartheta}=\exp\left(\ln N^{(2C\ln\vartheta)}\right)=N^{(2C\ln\vartheta)}.

In addition, if
C\geq\frac{1}{2\ln(\vartheta^{-1})}
, we have
2C\ln\vartheta\leq-1
. Hence, when
C\geq\frac{1}{2\ln(\vartheta^{-1})}
, we have

\max_{i}\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\epsilon_{it}|\epsilon_{i,pre},\epsilon_{i,post}\right]^{2}\right]\lesssim N^{-1}.\ \ \square

Claim 7
.

There is a constant
C>0
such that for all
i
,

\left|\gamma_{s}^{(i)}\right|\leq C\sigma_{u,i}^{2}\frac{\vartheta^{s}}{1-\vartheta^{2}}.

Proof of Claim
7
.
Because
\epsilon_{it}
is a causal AR process, we have the representation

\epsilon_{it}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}b^{(i)}_{k}u_{i,t-k}
where
\left|b^{(i)}_{k}\right|\leq c\vartheta^{k}
for some bounded constant
c>0
. Then, because

\gamma_{s}^{(i)}=\text{Cov}(\epsilon_{it},\epsilon_{i,t+s})=\text{Cov}\left(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}b^{(i)}_{k}u_{i,t-k},\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}b^{(i)}_{k}u_{i,t+s-k}\right)=\sigma_{u,i}^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}b^{(i)}_{k}b^{(i)}_{k+s},

we have

\left|\gamma_{s}^{(i)}\right|\leq c^{2}\sigma_{u,i}^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\vartheta^{k}\vartheta^{k+s}\leq c^{2}\sigma_{u,i}^{2}\vartheta^{s}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\vartheta^{2k}\leq C\sigma_{u,i}^{2}\frac{\vartheta^{s}}{1-\vartheta^{2}}.\ \ \square
